===== codex-docs/TRADE_INVESTIGATION_AGENT.md =====
   1: # Trade Investigation Agent
   2: 
   3: ## Role
   4: 
   5: Investigate one Crypto AI Trader trade from durable evidence. Explain what
   6: happened, why the system entered and exited, whether it followed the intended
   7: rules, and whether the entry/exit was supported by the information available
   8: at that time.
   9: 
  10: Investigations are read-only. Do not change code, configuration, services,
  11: database records, review states, Replay work, or deployment state.
  12: 
  13: ## Required input
  14: 
  15: Obtain or resolve:
  16: 
  17: - Environment and application: local, test, or production.
  18: - Database host, port, and schema.
  19: - One trade reference: `paper_position.id`, `wallet_trade.id`, or a symbol plus
  20:   a bounded approximate entry-time window.
  21: - Optional question or suspected issue.
  22: 
  23: Before analysis, confirm the symbol, trade/position ID, running version or FIX
  24: level, and ingestion mode. Read the actual schema before choosing tables or
  25: columns. Do not assume a table is in `crypto_ai` when it may belong to
  26: `crypto_ai_v2`.
  27: 
  28: ## Evidence rules
  29: 
  30: - Use configured database and SSH/log connections without displaying secrets.
  31: - Use bounded, read-only queries and targeted log searches.
  32: - Treat stored timestamps as UTC; display both UTC and KSA (`UTC+03:00`).
  33: - Attach record IDs and timestamped log evidence to every factual conclusion.
  34: - Distinguish **confirmed fact**, **supported inference**, and **missing or
  35:   conflicting evidence**.
  36: - Missing logs or records do not prove that no execution/effect occurred.
  37: - A saved signal does not prove execution. A callback return does not prove a
  38:   transaction committed. Under FIX-153, `market_price_event.APPLIED` proves a
  39:   committed successful protection callback, but can be a no-op when there was
  40:   no open position; find the position/trade mutation separately.
  41: - Current configuration does not prove which settings were active at entry. If
  42:   no configuration history exists, report that limitation.
  43: 
  44: ## Investigation workflow
  45: 
  46: ### 1. Resolve identity and environment
  47: 
  48: Link the trade reference to, where available:
  49: 
  50: - `paper_position`
  51: - `wallet_trade`
  52: - entry and exit `trade_signal`
  53: - `signal_processing_work`
  54: - wallet/position execution records
  55: 
  56: Record entry and exit times in UTC and KSA. Confirm whether the trader ran in
  57: local-candle OFF mode or a legacy shared-market mode.
  58: 
  59: ### 2. Collect a small evidence bundle
  60: 
  61: Start with exact IDs and a narrow time window. Expand only when the evidence
  62: requires it. Collect:
  63: 
  64: | Evidence | Establishes |
  65: | --- | --- |
  66: | `paper_position` | lifecycle, entry/exit, stop, target, status |
  67: | `wallet_trade` | executed quantity, prices, fees, linked signal |
  68: | `trade_signal` | decision, strategy, scores, entry eligibility, reasons |
  69: | `signal_processing_work` | claim, attempt, completion, expiry, uncertainty |
  70: | `candle` / `technical_indicator` | market data and analysis inputs available then |
  71: | `market_price_event` | intra-candle protection observations and outcomes |
  72: | configuration/version evidence | applicable rules, when historically available |
  73: | application and timing logs | analysis, execution, protection, exceptions, waits |
  74: 
  75: Avoid fetching large signal explanation fields until they are needed.
  76: 
  77: ### 3. Reconstruct the timeline
  78: 
  79: Build a timeline, explicitly showing missing stages:
  80: 
  81: `candle closed Г+' signal saved Г+' work claimed Г+' locks acquired Г+' entry committed Г+' protection observations Г+' exit decision Г+' exit committed`
  82: 
  83: Measure separately:
  84: 
  85: - candle close to signal generation;
  86: - signal generation to processing/entry;
  87: - observation time to protection completion;
  88: - exit-trigger evidence to recorded exit.
  89: 
  90: Correlate delays only with logs/events for the same symbol, position, signal,
  91: or price-event identity. A nearby slow SQL query is not proof of causation.
  92: 
  93: ### 4. Assess entry correctness and quality
  94: 
  95: Use only evidence that was available at entry. Check:
  96: 
  97: - signal mode, closed-candle identity, freshness, and data quality;
  98: - final decision and entry-allowed result;
  99: - BTC context, higher-timeframe, ATR, liquidity, risk/reward, sizing, and
 100:   strategy gates that applied at that time;
 101: - correct signal/position linkage and absence of duplicate/stale-retry entry;
 102: - quantity, wallet balance, risk limits, fees, and fill/reference-price gap.
 103: 
 104: Return two separate conclusions:
 105: 
 106: - **Execution correctness:** followed or violated the implemented rules.
 107: - **Entry quality:** supported, weak, or unverifiable from contemporaneous
 108:   evidence.
 109: 
 110: Subsequent price movement may evaluate the outcome; it must never be presented
 111: as information the entry decision had.
 112: 
 113: ### 5. Reconstruct protection and stop-loss behavior
 114: 
 115: For FIX-153, inspect accepted `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` price events and retain
 116: `PENDING`, `NON_CANONICAL`, and `PROTECTION_NOT_APPLIED` records as evidence
 117: gaps or exclusions. Never substitute candle OHLC for absent intra-candle price
 118: evidence.
 119: 
 120: For each relevant accepted observation:
 121: 
 122: 1. Determine the then-active stop, target, trailing stop, and profit-lock state.
 123: 2. Find the first accepted observation that met an exit threshold.
 124: 3. Confirm that protection evaluated it and identify the resulting position or
 125:    wallet execution record.
 126: 4. Explain delays, rejections, stale/out-of-order classification, exceptions,
 127:    lock waits, or pool pressure.
 128: 
 129: Classify the exit path accurately: fixed stop-loss, trailing stop, profit-lock,
 130: take-profit, signal-driven exit, manual exit, or unknown.
 131: 
 132: ### 6. Assess exit correctness and quality
 133: 
 134: Compare the recorded exit reason, quantity, and price with trigger evidence.
 135: Check threshold reach, accepted-observation identity, protection timing, and
 136: the committed execution. Calculate maximum favourable/adverse movement during
 137: the holding period separately from the contemporaneous exit assessment.
 138: 
 139: ### 7. Reconcile financial results
 140: 
 141: Recalculate using execution records:
 142: 
 143: - bought/sold quantities and weighted entry/exit prices;
 144: - gross P&L, fees, and net P&L;
 145: - partial exits, remaining quantity, and realized versus unrealized values.
 146: 
 147: Flag discrepancies among wallet records, position records, and UI output.
 148: 
 149: ### 8. Diagnose cause and use Replay carefully
 150: 
 151: Classify findings as one or more of:
 152: 
 153: - strategy decision;
 154: - data freshness or coverage;
 155: - analysis delay;
 156: - execution failure;
 157: - protection failure;
 158: - database, lock, or connection-pool contention;
 159: - accounting/UI discrepancy;
 160: - insufficient evidence.
 161: 
 162: Run Replay only as a controlled comparison with equivalent candles, accepted
 163: prices, strategy version, and settings. State every unavailable input or
 164: material difference. Never claim parity by substituting candle OHLC for missing
 165: price evidence or treating `PENDING` as accepted.
 166: 
 167: ## Required final report
 168: 
 169: 1. **Trade identity and environment** Г?" IDs, symbol, version/mode, scope.
 170: 2. **UTC/KSA timeline** Г?" evidence-backed sequence and latency breakdown.
 171: 3. **Entry** Г?" execution correctness and entry quality.
 172: 4. **Exit** Г?" execution correctness and exit quality.
 173: 5. **Stop-loss/profit-lock** Г?" worked, failed, or unverifiable, with evidence.
 174: 6. **P&L reconciliation** Г?" quantities, prices, fees, and discrepancies.
 175: 7. **Root cause and confidence** Г?" confirmed defect, valid loss, configuration
 176:    issue, delay, or insufficient evidence.
 177: 8. **Evidence references** Г?" record IDs, query/log timestamps, code/config
 178:    references.
 179: 9. **Missing evidence** Г?" exactly what prevents a stronger conclusion.
 180: 10. **Recommended correction and regression checks** Г?" only when justified.
 181: 
 182: A profitable trade can violate the rules, and a losing trade can be a correct
 183: execution. State those independently.
===== codex-docs/TRADING_RULES.md =====
   1: # FIX-154 higher-timeframe BUY rule Г?" 2026-10-10
   2: 
   3: - `data_quality_valid=true` is required before a bearish 1h signal can be
   4:   treated as trusted. `NULL` is unknown and fails closed for bearish evidence.
   5: - A trusted `STRONG_SELL` at `confidence_score >= 70` blocks the BUY.
   6: - A trusted `SELL`, or trusted `STRONG_SELL` below 70, is a SOFT conflict:
   7:   profiles receive effective 1h `NEUTRAL`, and any allowed initial BUY is
   8:   capped at 25%.
   9: - A bearish 5m still returns `5M_BEARISH_VETO` before any 1h result.
  10: - Existing progressive/add rules retain their raw 1h requirement; a SOFT 1h
  11:   never authorizes an add.
  12: 
  13: # FIX-153 candle continuity rule Г?" 2026-10-10
  14: 
  15: - Missing price evidence never becomes invented OHLCV data and never bypasses
  16:   the data-quality gate.
  17: - Automatic reconciliation may restore only one exact, final Binance candle.
  18:   Its recovered/backfilled row has no close token and cannot directly cause
  19:   analysis, a signal, or a trade.
  20: - A later normal live close is the only route that resumes ordinary signal
  21:   generation after a repaired quality window.
===== codex-docs/INVESTIGATION_LOG.md =====
   1: # Local candle coverage check Г?" 2026-10-09
   2: 
   3: - Scope: read-only check of all 21 enabled symbols in `crypto_ai.candle`, over the two-hour window ending at the database UTC clock.
   4: - Current state: all 63 enabled symbol/interval lanes (1m, 5m, 1h) have closed-candle records and current live ingestion. The newest 1m candle was 54 seconds old at the check.
   5: - Continuity: 5m and 1h had no internal gaps in the inspected window. Twelve symbols had one missing 1m candle: the interval from `2026-10-08T22:18:00Z` to `2026-10-08T22:19:00Z` (KSA `01:18` to `01:19`). This coincides with the local application startup period; the check establishes the gap but does not prove its cause.
   6: - Impact: current live signals are being generated, but the missing historical 1m candles should be reconciled before relying on uninterrupted historical indicator lineage for that startup window. No database data or code was changed by this check.
   7: 
   8: # Signal-staleness root cause Г?" 2026-10-09
   9: 
  10: - System Health CRITICAL signal-staleness alerts were confirmed as accurate for 1m and 5m analysis, not a dashboard timezone issue. At the check, candles continued through `2026-10-09T06:43:00Z` (1m) and `06:35:00Z` (5m), while the newest live signals were `04:34:03Z` (1m) and `04:30:07Z` (5m).
  11: - The most recent 210-candle data-quality window has a synchronized gap for every enabled symbol: missing 1m open `2026-10-09T04:34:00Z` (KSA 07:34) and missing 5m open `2026-10-09T04:30:00Z` (KSA 07:30).
  12: - Cause confirmed from the WebSocket lifecycle log: generation 1 was revoked at `2026-10-09T04:35:00.133951100Z`; generation 2 was admitted at `04:35:01.369103400Z` (1,236 ms interruption). The affected 1m and 5m candles had both closed at the reconnect boundary. Binance did not replay their final closed-kline events, leaving the already-created local rows open rather than finalized.
  13: - `CandleDataQualityService` is configured with `minimum-candles: 210` and `maximum-missing-candles: 0`. Therefore one missing candle makes the entire relevant analysis window invalid. Runtime logs confirm `DATA_QUALITY_BLOCKED` with `Missing 1 candle(s) in the analysis window` for current 1m and 5m worker attempts.
  14: - 1h has no comparable gap and produced a live signal at `2026-10-09T06:00:03Z` (KSA 09:00).
  15: - No repair or configuration change was made. Correct remediation must restore authoritative missing candles rather than relaxing the zero-missing data-quality safety rule.
  16: 
  17: ## Bounded local repair completed
  18: 
  19: - Authorized local repair finalized the 42 interrupted `RUNTIME` rows using Binance REST candles for the exact identities above: 21 x 1m and 21 x 5m.
  20: - Each response was validated for the expected open and close timestamp before one transaction finalized only a row where `closed=0`. Existing closed candles, provenance, and close-transition tokens were not changed; no close-dispatch, analysis, signal, or trade was initiated by the repair.
  21: - Verification: all 1m/5m 210-candle quality windows had zero gaps after the repair. The next live close produced new LIVE signals at `2026-10-09T07:30:07Z` for both 1m and 5m.
  22: 
  23: # ETHUSDT paper-trade 59 investigation Г?" 2026-10-09
  24: 
  25: - Scope: local `crypto_ai` paper trade `wallet_trade.id=59` for `ETHUSDT`; all database reads were bounded and read-only. Stored UTC timestamps are shown below with their KSA (`UTC+03:00`) interpretation.
  26: - Entry: signal `trade_signal.id=69226` was generated at `2026-10-09T07:33:00.233691Z` (10:33:00.234 KSA) from the 1m candle opened at 10:32 KSA. It was a `BUY`, score 79, confidence 70, entry allowed, at 2502.77. The trade executed at `07:33:01.654548Z` (10:33:01.655 KSA), 1.42 seconds after the signal; this is not an execution-latency issue.
  27: - Exit: `wallet_trade.id=60` closed the position at 2504.30 at `07:34:34.196100Z` (10:34:34.196 KSA) with `PROFIT_LOCK_EXIT`. The managed position recorded a high of 2505.80 and an activated profit-lock price of 2504.429502243252. The realized paper gain was about 0.02445 USDT before fees (0.015982291620 ETH x 1.53 USDT).
  28: - Market path: ETH rose from 2485.88 at 07:54 KSA to 2502.77 at entry (+16.89, +0.68%). It reached 2505.56 at 10:25 KSA before the entry; entry was 2.79 (0.11%) below that high. The post-entry high was 2505.93 at 10:34 KSA, only 3.16 (0.13%) above entry, then the candle retraced. This supports the user's observation that entry occurred late in the observed move.
  29: - Primary cause: the local candle-quality interruption stopped 1m ETH signals after 07:34 KSA. There were no ETH 1m signals from 07:34 to 10:26 KSA, the period containing most of the rise. The interrupted 1m and 5m rows were repaired at about 10:28 KSA, so analysis resumed only near the later consolidation/breakout attempt. This is a confirmed data-availability gap, not proof that the strategy would have bought near 07:54 KSA.
  30: - Immediate signal sequence after recovery: at 10:30 KSA the 5m signal was a BUY but blocked by `RANGE_ENTRY_LOCATION`; at 10:31 KSA the 1m raw BUY was vetoed by a bearish BTC context; at 10:33 KSA BTC context became neutral and the 1m trend-following BUY was allowed. The entry signal itself explicitly recorded mixed higher-timeframe context (5m BUY, 1h NEUTRAL) and a weakening but ultra-close ask wall before its target.
  31: - Assessment: the trade followed the implemented rules once local analysis resumed, and the trade was entered/exited promptly. It was nevertheless a late entry relative to the full 07:54Г?"10:33 KSA advance because the system lacked signal coverage during the main move. The data gap is an operational defect already covered by the local-candle recovery deployment gate. Whether the unobserved earlier period would have produced an executable BUY is uncertain without a controlled Replay run; none was started because that would create replay state.
  32: # SHIBUSDT signal #89469 / position #35 Г?" 2026-10-10
  33: 
  34: - Scope: read-only local `crypto_ai` investigation of wallet trades `106`/`107`,
  35:   paper position `35`, managed position `64`, and signal `89469`.
  36: - Timestamp correction (2026-10-10): the preceding interpretation was wrong
  37:   because the read-only investigation connection used MySQL's `SYSTEM` timezone
  38:   (KSA), while the application connection explicitly sets its session to UTC.
  39:   For position `35`, MySQL's KSA session renders `opened_at` as
  40:   `2026-10-09 23:45:06.682175`; converting that instant to UTC yields
  41:   `2026-10-09 20:45:06.682175`. The Inspector's main trade card parses backend
  42:   timestamps as UTC and renders them in the browser-local timezone. Therefore,
  43:   with a KSA browser, its displayed 23:45 entry time is correct. Do not add
  44:   three hours to the value shown by this KSA-session SQL connection.
  45: - Signal confirmation: `trade_signal.id=89469` was generated at
  46:   `2026-10-09 23:45:03.498678` KSA (`20:45:03.498678` UTC), from the 1m
  47:   candle opened at 23:44 KSA. It is `WATCH`, score 60, confidence 74,
  48:   `WEAK_UPTREND`, `TREND_FOLLOWING`, and `final_entry_allowed=1`.
  49: - Entry: `0.000005410`; stop `0.000005382950`; TP `0.000005433730`; the
  50:   25%-size setup-wakeup route entered from a 5m BUY with 1h NEUTRAL context.
  51: - Exit: highest recorded price before close was `0.000005430`, or 84.2815% of
  52:   the original TP distance. The dynamic lock activated at `0.000005421865` and
  53:   live price `0.000005420` breached it. At the breach the current 1m and 5m
  54:   were WATCH and 1h was NEUTRAL. The shared exit policy therefore returned
  55:   `PROFIT_LOCK_EXIT`; it requires bullish 1h plus supportive 5m, or WATCH 1h
  56:   plus supportive 5m, to hold a breached lock.
  57: - TP extension: not evaluated. Extension begins only once price is at or above
  58:   the original TP. The recorded high remained below TP at the time of exit.
  59: - Subsequent action: later local candles reached `0.000005500` at about 00:40
  60:   KSA, above the original TP. This is hindsight, not evidence available at the
  61:   exit. Local SHIB 1m/5m coverage ends at about 00:56 KSA, so any claimed
  62:   continuation through 12:15 KSA is not verifiable from local candle evidence.
  63: - Assessment: behavior followed the existing protection rule, without a

  66:   measurable momentum/structure conditions and a tighter safety floor; it

===== codex-docs/ARCHITECTURE.md lines 1-285 =====
   1: # Crypto AI Trader ƒ?" Architecture
   2: 
   3: Status labels used in this document:
   4: 
   5: - **Current** ƒ?" present in the repository today.
   6: - **Pending** ƒ?" approved design direction, not implemented or deployed.
   7: - **Legacy/shared** ƒ?" existing shared-market path retained as historical evidence
   8:   while the trader moves to local-candle ownership.
   9: 
  10: ## Current release status
  11: 
  12: **FIX-153 is implemented in source but is NOT APPROVED FOR PRODUCTION.**
  13: It moves the trader toward collector-free, trader-owned local candle ingestion
  14: in `shared-market.mode=OFF`. Java 21, disposable-MySQL, crash/race, Windows
  15: two-process, soak, OFF-mode and Replay-parity proof gates remain open.
  16: 
  17: FIX-154 is a separate pending higher-timeframe design. It is not implemented
  18: and is not part of FIX-153 deployment scope.
  19: 
  20: ## System purpose
  21: 
  22: The application is a Spring Boot cryptocurrency trader that analyses Binance
  23: market data, persists candles and technical indicators, creates trade signals,
  24: and performs paper-wallet execution and position protection. Production and
  25: Replay are intended to use equivalent persisted market evidence.
  26: 
  27: ## Runtime and data ownership
  28: 
  29: | Area | Current ownership | Target direction |
  30: |---|---|---|
  31: | Trader database | `crypto_ai` | Remains the trader's only runtime database. |
  32: | Collector database | `crypto_ai_v2` | Collector remains separate and is not read/written by the trader at runtime. |
  33: | Candle ingestion | In OFF mode, trader-owned Binance ingestion writes local `crypto_ai.candle`. | Must be proven in a collector-free deployment rehearsal. |
  34: | Candle processing | Bounded lanes, close-transition tokens and a local detector are implemented. | MySQL/race/crash proof remains required. |
  35: | Trading | Paper wallet and managed positions. | Live-exchange orders are outside the local-candle automatic-resolution design. |
  36: 
  37: ## Current primary flow
  38: 
  39: ```text
  40: Binance WebSocket kline
  41:   -> BinanceKlineService
  42:   -> KlineTransactionCoordinator
  43:       -> input transaction: candle + canonical 1m market price event
  44:       -> protection transaction: LivePositionProtectionService
  45:       -> observer transaction
  46:       -> close-dispatch transaction
  47:   -> CandleClosedEvent (AFTER_COMMIT)
  48:   -> CandleAnalysisDispatcher
  49:   -> CandleClosedAnalysisWorker
  50:   -> TechnicalIndicatorService
  51:   -> AnalysisService / TradeSignal
  52:   -> SignalProcessingCoordinator
  53:   -> PaperTradingService / wallet and managed-position state
  54: ```
  55: 
  56: `KlineTransactionCoordinator` preserves input -> protection -> observer ->
  57: close-dispatch ordering. Closed-candle analysis is asynchronous and must not
  58: run on the Binance ingestion thread.
  59: 
  60: ## Current core persistence
  61: 
  62: | Table/domain | Purpose |
  63: |---|---|
  64: | `candle` | Local OHLCV candle history keyed by symbol, interval, and open time. |
  65: | `technical_indicator` | Persisted indicator snapshots derived from closed candles. |
  66: | `trade_signal` | Persisted analysis decisions and their context. |
  67: | `signal_processing_work` | Durable processing registration/status for a persisted signal. |
  68: | `market_price_event` | Canonical 1m price evidence used by protection and Replay. |
  69: | Paper wallet / position tables | Paper executions, open-position state, TP/SL/profit protection. |
  70: 
  71: ## Legacy/shared-market path
  72: 
  73: **Current but pending retirement from the trader runtime.**
  74: 
  75: The shared path includes `SharedMarketSource`, `SharedMarketConsumer`,
  76: `SharedAnalysisDispatcher`, cutover/readiness logic, and local historical
  77: tables such as `shared_market_event_delivery`, `shared_market_consumer_state`,
  78: and `shared_market_health`. These records remain evidence and must not be
  79: dropped, reset, or used to manufacture an approval.
  80: 
  81: The collector event/cursor source is `crypto_ai_v2.market_data_stream_event`
  82: and `market_data_stream_cursor`. In the target local-candle runtime, the
  83: trader must not connect to, read, or write these collector tables.
  84: 
  85: `V103__fix_149_fresh_admission_indexes` is an existing Java Flyway migration
  86: that requires the collector event table for a fresh installation. A trader-only
  87: bootstrap policy is therefore **pending** and remains a release blocker. Do
  88: not edit applied Flyway migrations or Flyway history.
  89: 
  90: ## Implemented, unverified: local-candle architecture (FIX-153)
  91: 
  92: The implemented intended mode is `shared-market.mode=OFF` with trader-owned Binance kline
  93: ingestion and local candle writes. The shared executors and direct-ingestion
  94: path must never run together.
  95: 
  96: Implemented safeguards:
  97: 
  98: - Candle writes are monotonic: a late open update cannot reopen or overwrite a
  99:   closed candle.
 100: - A closed-candle transition is admitted exactly once using an atomic upsert
 101:   result plus in-process admission coordination.
 102: - A bounded local detector recovers a closed candle that was committed before
 103:   the in-memory event/dispatcher could start analysis.
 104: - Fresh candles may execute; stale candles are analysed historically only, or
 105:   their already-owned work is expired without business execution.
 106: - Analysis lanes are bounded. Under overload, 1m may retain only the latest
 107:   pending candle; higher intervals use an explicit bounded FIFO policy.
 108: - Historic shared `REVIEW_REQUIRED` evidence must not block OFF-mode work.
 109: - OFF-mode health/admin endpoints must use local-candle health and must not
 110:   query retired shared-market tables.
 111: - Current REST backfill can finalise an interrupted open row but cannot mint a
 112:   runtime close token or dispatch analysis. Runtime close tokens plus
 113:   provenance keep backfill out of detector admission. The approved, pending
 114:   automatic-reconciliation extension will also insert an exact wholly absent
 115:   candle only inside the active quality window, with `BACKFILL` provenance and
 116:   the same no-token/no-dispatch boundary.
 117: - Observed local behavior (2026-10-09): a WebSocket reconnect at a candle-close
 118:   boundary can leave the already-persisted 1m/5m rows open because Binance does
 119:   not replay the final closed-kline event. The current detector recovers missing
 120:   analysis admission, not missing close facts. An authoritative, bounded REST
 121:   repair may therefore finalise the exact `closed=0` row or, under the approved
 122:   gap-backfill scope, insert an exact absent identity. It must preserve existing
 123:   provenance, use `BACKFILL` for an insertion, create no close-transition token,
 124:   and never dispatch historical work. Automatic reconciliation of this reconnect
 125:   case remains a FIX-153 release gate; the one-time local repair is evidence,
 126:   not proof that the gap is closed.
 127: - Approved pending reconciliation design: gap backfill is insert-only (no
 128:   duplicate-key update); it can never alter a live row that appears during a
 129:   REST request. Candidate selection is bounded to the active quality window;
 130:   terminal audit records are not retried. Every candle/work route must produce
 131:   a durable terminal outcome or remain explicitly retryable after a transient
 132:   failure. Historical-only processing has no execution authority.
 133: - The pending universal mutation barrier is an in-memory drain participant,
 134:   not a database or persistent trading fence. Outside drain it must not delay,
 135:   queue, or block fresh trading, signals, or protection. Open-position
 136:   protection during application replacement remains an unresolved release gate.
 137: - Local source now contains the first reconciliation implementation slice:
 138:   V107 durable audit/outcome tables, update-only authoritative open-row repair,
 139:   pure insert-only gap backfill, a dedicated recovery executor, a reconnect
 140:   request hook, and drain participation. It is disabled by default and remains
 141:   unvalidated on MySQL; it has no deployment approval.
 142: - Direct prices are normalised to `DECIMAL(30,12)` before persistence,
 143:   protection and completion. Equal timestamps have one canonical receipt.
 144: - Every processing attempt is freshness-checked after normal locks using the
 145:   database-to-exchange correction. JVM-origin timestamp decisions use the
 146:   separately measured JVM-to-exchange correction. The measurement performs no
 147:   HTTP under execution locks and uses a bounded five-second time-only client.
 148:   General Binance REST calls use the configured request timeout, and scheduled
 149:   clock sampling has a four-thread Spring scheduler pool.
 150:   Bootstrap obtains time/klines before opening its local candle-write
 151:   transaction, so a stalled REST request does not retain database resources.
 152:   REST bootstrap gets Binance server time before klines.
 153: - An ADMIN-only drain stops analysis admission, exits the JVM on successful
 154:   quiesce, and resumes admission on timeout. Price protection pauses during
 155:   the controlled drain window.
 156: 
 157: ## Implemented, unverified: protection price-event state machine
 158: 
 159: For direct 1m price observations, the target evidence lifecycle is:
 160: 
 161: ```text
 162: PENDING
 163:   -> APPLIED                 (protection effects and state commit together)
 164:   -> NON_CANONICAL           (invalid, stale, out-of-order, or duplicate)
 165:   -> PROTECTION_NOT_APPLIED  (confirmed rollback/crash resolution)
 166: ```
 167: 
 168: Replay and price authority consume only accepted `LEGACY`/`APPLIED` rows.
 169: Replay must reject a parity claim when the requested range contains unresolved
 170: direct `PENDING` evidence; this must not block live trading.
 171: 
 172: The automatic resolver is valid only for the paper-trading phase, where every
 173: protection effect commits in the same transaction as its `APPLIED` update.
 174: Before deployment, the code must prove that there are no asynchronous,
 175: `REQUIRES_NEW`, in-memory-only, or external effects in this boundary. A future
 176: live-exchange order needs a separate idempotent/exchange-reconciliation design.
 177: 
 178: The target runtime also requires exactly one active trader process. This is an
 179: operational prerequisite, not a database lease or fence.
 180: 
 181: ## Pending: FIX-154 higher-timeframe BUY validation
 182: 
 183: FIX-154 is separate from the local-candle change and is not implemented.
 184: 
 185: Its purpose is to let a historical bearish 1h signal affect a current 1m BUY
 186: only when the historical evidence is fresh, quality-valid, and sufficiently
 187: strong. `btcContextEntryAllowed=false` on a historical signal is investigatory
 188: evidence, not a higher-timeframe quality gate; the current 1m BTC protection
 189: remains unchanged.
 190: 
 191: The design includes reliability bands (`NOT_TRUSTED`, `SOFT_CONFLICT`,
 192: `HARD_BEARISH_CANDIDATE`), required fresh/quality-valid bearish 5m support for
 193: a hard veto, persisted `trade_signal.data_quality_valid`, recovery release
 194: rules, immutable outcome audit evidence, and Replay/no-look-ahead parity.
 195: 
 196: The implementation design is maintained in `codex-docs/FIX-154_TECHNICAL_DESIGN.md`.
 197: It supersedes no existing deployed behavior and is deliberately separate from
 198: the unrelated legacy readiness document under `src/main/resources/md/FIX-146.md`.
 199: 
 200: ## Deployment posture
 201: 
 202: Neither FIX-153 nor FIX-154 is deployment-ready. FIX-153 source hardening is
 203: implemented but unverified; FIX-154 remains pending.
 204: 
 205: Required validation includes Java 21 build/tests, disposable MySQL bootstrap,
 206: real-MySQL concurrency and lock-wait tests, crash/restart tests, stale-input
 207: tests, Replay parity checks, OFF-mode health/admin checks, collector-free
 208: runtime verification, deterministic dispatcher/detector/drain tests,
 209: two-process Windows lock rehearsal, and sustained-load observation across
 210: enabled symbols and intervals. This local workspace has Java 17 and cannot
 211: compile the Java 21 project.
 212: 
 213: No production configuration, database, service, or deployment change is made
 214: without explicit user approval.
 215: 
 216: ## FIX-153 validation status (2026-10-09)
 217: 
 218: The reconciliation component now has deterministic Java 21 coverage for its
 219: core admission and outcome contract. The tests execute the real audit and
 220: outcome stores against H2 while mocking only Binance and the candle write
 221: boundary. They prove that healthy delayed live closes are not taken over and
 222: that recovery remains token-free/non-live when it is positively evidenced.
 223: 
 224: The real-MySQL suite contains matching update-only and insert-only assertions,
 225: but it requires a deliberately disposable loopback MySQL instance and is not
 226: run by the ordinary unit-test goal. This is a release gate, not a warning that
 227: can be waived. The shared Binance 429/418 admission gate, universal mutation
 228: barrier, structural historical-only isolation, financial-effect reconciler,
 229: atomicity/crash proofs, replacement-time position protection, Replay parity,
 230: and soak validation remain incomplete. Therefore `local-candle.reconciliation.enabled`
 231: must stay `false`.
 232: 
 233: The single `local-candle` configuration mapping is a startup invariant. It now
 234: contains both reconciliation and existing local-runtime properties; a second
 235: mapping is invalid YAML and prevents the Spring application context from
 236: starting.
 237: 
 238: ### Shared Binance REST admission
 239: 
 240: `BinanceRestAdmissionGate` owns one process-wide REST cooldown. It is consulted
 241: immediately before every call through `BinanceMarketDataClient`; a 429/418
 242: response extends the cooldown using `Retry-After` when it is supplied, otherwise
 243: the bounded fallback. This protects all local REST consumers from repeatedly
 244: calling Binance during a rate-limit/ban response. It is deliberately not part
 245: of the WebSocket or protection path and is not a persisted trading fence.
 246: 
 247: ### Outcome-schema startup invariant
 248: 
 249: The active signal-processing runtime writes `candle_analysis_outcome` for
 250: terminal analysis evidence. V108 therefore ships with the FIX-154 release even
 251: though local-candle reconciliation remains disabled. `CandleAnalysisOutcomeSchemaVerifier`
 252: runs after Flyway and fails application startup with a clear message if the
 253: table is absent; this prevents later processing transactions from failing only
 254: when they attempt their first outcome write.
 255: # FIX-154 implementation update ƒ?" 2026-10-10
 256: 
 257: `HigherTimeframeBuyAssessmentService` is the single pure assessment boundary for
 258: the 5m/1h BUY context. It returns effective decisions separately from the JPA
 259: entities, so a SOFT 1h conflict can be assessed as effective `NEUTRAL` without
 260: ever changing the persisted signal. `TradeExecutionValidationService` uses it
 261: for direct BUY and accumulated-evidence authority; `OpportunityConsolidationService`
 262: uses the same assessment for consolidation. A trusted SOFT bearish 1h limits an
 263: initial entry to 25%; a trusted HARD or unknown bearish 1h rejects it, while
 264: the legacy 5m bearish veto keeps priority.
 265: 
 266: `buy_htf_assessment_audit` is append-only operational evidence. Its writer is
 267: best-effort and uses a bounded dedicated executor: auditing never borrows an
 268: execution transaction's database connection, is skipped in Replay, and cannot
 269: be part of a BUY decision. The progressive add route retains its
 270: existing raw-1h guard; it writes `SOFT_HTF_ADD_BLOCKED` evidence only after
 271: that guard has already rejected an add.
 272: 
 273: `GET /api/fix154/health` exposes non-gating counters for untrusted bearish
 274: blocks, soft caps, soft add blocks, and best-effort audit failures.
 275: 
 276: ## FIX-153 automatic local-candle reconciliation ƒ?" enabled 2026-10-10
 277: 
 278: In `shared-market.mode=OFF`, `LocalCandleReconciliationService` runs every
 279: five seconds on its dedicated single-worker executor. It finds eligible
 280: missing or interrupted candle identities inside the data-quality window, reads
 281: the authoritative Binance candle, and writes only verified final evidence.
 282: An existing open row is finalized by an update-only path; a missing row uses a
 283: plain insert-if-absent backfill path. Neither route creates a close token,
 284: dispatches analysis, or executes a trade. The next genuine live close resumes
 285: ordinary analysis after the quality window is complete.

  14: 
  15: - Problem: JVM/database clock skew could make a Binance-derived candle appear
  16:   too early or too old during freshness checks.
  17: - Solution: `ExchangeClockSynchronizer` measures Binance server time against
  18:   database UTC and JVM time at startup and periodically; database and JVM
  19:   callers apply their own bounded offset without HTTP inside locks.
  20: - The clock-skew warning uses the larger absolute database or JVM offset; the
  21:   dedicated Binance time client has a five-second connect/read bound.
  22: - General Binance REST requests now use the configured request timeout, and
  23:   scheduled clock sampling cannot monopolise the four-thread scheduler pool.
  24: - Bootstrap REST reads occur before its local candle-write transaction, so
  25:   the bounded Binance timeout does not hold a database transaction open.

 111: 
 112: - Recovery now requires positive loss evidence before it may finalise an open
 113:   row: either the successor candle is already present or a reconnect was

===== src/main/java/com/crypto/position/service/PositionExitPolicy.java =====
   1: package com.crypto.position.service;
   2: 
   3: import com.crypto.domain.SignalDecision;
   4: import com.crypto.domain.TradeSignal;
   5: import org.springframework.stereotype.Component;
   6: 
   7: /**
   8:  * Shared production/replay exit authority for an already-open long position.
   9:  *
  10:  * The policy deliberately separates short-timeframe noise from a genuine
  11:  * higher-timeframe breakdown:
  12:  *  - a 1m pullback alone never closes the position;
  13:  *  - the existing 1m SELL + bearish 5m confirmation remains valid;
  14:  *  - a fresh 1h SELL may close a position when 5m is no longer supportive;
  15:  *  - a Profit Lock breach is tolerated while 5m/1h still support the bullish thesis.
  16:  */
  17: @Component
  18: public class PositionExitPolicy {
  19: 
  20:     public Evaluation evaluateNormalExit(TradeSignal current, TradeSignal fiveMinute, TradeSignal oneHour) {
  21:         SignalDecision currentDecision = decision(current);
  22:         SignalDecision fiveDecision = decision(fiveMinute);
  23:         SignalDecision oneDecision = decision(oneHour);
  24: 
  25:         boolean oneHourBreakdown = bearish(oneDecision)
  26:                 && fiveMinute != null
  27:                 && !supportive(fiveDecision);
  28:         if (oneHourBreakdown) {
  29:             return Evaluation.exit("HTF_SELL_CONFIRMED",
  30:                     "1h turned " + label(oneDecision)
  31:                             + " while 5m is no longer supportive (" + label(fiveDecision)
  32:                             + "). The higher-timeframe bullish thesis is broken; close the managed position.");
  33:         }
  34: 
  35:         boolean lowerFrameConfirmed = current != null
  36:                 && "1m".equals(current.getInterval())
  37:                 && bearish(currentDecision)
  38:                 && fiveMinute != null
  39:                 && bearish(fiveDecision)
  40:                 && (oneHour == null || !bullish(oneDecision));
  41:         if (lowerFrameConfirmed) {
  42:             return Evaluation.exit("SELL_CONFIRMED",
  43:                     "1m " + label(currentDecision) + " is confirmed by bearish 5m while 1h is not bullish.");
  44:         }
  45: 
  46:         return Evaluation.hold("No confirmed multi-timeframe exit."
  47:                 + " current=" + label(currentDecision)
  48:                 + ", 5m=" + label(fiveDecision)
  49:                 + ", 1h=" + label(oneDecision) + ".");
  50:     }
  51: 
  52:     public Evaluation evaluateProfitLockBreach(TradeSignal current, TradeSignal fiveMinute, TradeSignal oneHour) {
  53:         SignalDecision currentDecision = decision(current);
  54:         SignalDecision fiveDecision = decision(fiveMinute);
  55:         SignalDecision oneDecision = decision(oneHour);
  56: 
  57:         // Strong HTF support: tolerate a sharp 1m pullback instead of throwing away the trend.
  58:         if (bullish(oneDecision) && fiveMinute != null && supportive(fiveDecision)) {
  59:             return Evaluation.hold("PROFIT_LOCK_HOLD",
  60:                     "Profit Lock was breached, but higher-timeframe continuation remains healthy:"
  61:                             + " 1h=" + label(oneDecision) + ", 5m=" + label(fiveDecision)
  62:                             + ", current=" + label(currentDecision)
  63:                             + ". Treat the breach as a protected pullback, not an immediate SELL.");
  64:         }
  65: 
  66:         // Cooling but still-supported trend: WATCH/WATCH is allowed to breathe while the current frame is not bearish.
  67:         if (oneDecision == SignalDecision.WATCH
  68:                 && fiveMinute != null && supportive(fiveDecision)
  69:                 && !bearish(currentDecision)) {
  70:             return Evaluation.hold("PROFIT_LOCK_HOLD",
  71:                     "Profit Lock was breached during a supported consolidation:"
  72:                             + " 1h=WATCH, 5m=" + label(fiveDecision)
  73:                             + ", current=" + label(currentDecision)
  74:                             + ". Keep the winner protected while waiting for higher-timeframe confirmation.");
  75:         }
  76: 
  77:         if (bearish(oneDecision)) {
  78:             return Evaluation.exit("PROFIT_LOCK_HTF_EXIT",
  79:                     "Profit Lock was breached and 1h is " + label(oneDecision)
  80:                             + "; higher-timeframe support has failed.");
  81:         }
  82: 
  83:         if (bearish(currentDecision) && bearish(fiveDecision)) {
  84:             return Evaluation.exit("PROFIT_LOCK_BEARISH_CONFIRMATION",
  85:                     "Profit Lock was breached with bearish 1m and 5m confirmation.");
  86:         }
  87: 
  88:         return Evaluation.exit("PROFIT_LOCK_EXIT",
  89:                 "Profit Lock was breached without enough higher-timeframe bullish support to justify a protected hold:"
  90:                         + " current=" + label(currentDecision)
  91:                         + ", 5m=" + label(fiveDecision)
  92:                         + ", 1h=" + label(oneDecision) + ".");
  93:     }
  94: 
  95:     private SignalDecision decision(TradeSignal signal) {
  96:         return signal == null ? null : signal.getDecision();
  97:     }
  98: 
  99:     private boolean supportive(SignalDecision decision) {
 100:         return decision == SignalDecision.BUY
 101:                 || decision == SignalDecision.STRONG_BUY
 102:                 || decision == SignalDecision.WATCH;
 103:     }
 104: 
 105:     private boolean bullish(SignalDecision decision) {
 106:         return decision == SignalDecision.BUY || decision == SignalDecision.STRONG_BUY;
 107:     }
 108: 
 109:     private boolean bearish(SignalDecision decision) {
 110:         return decision == SignalDecision.SELL || decision == SignalDecision.STRONG_SELL;
 111:     }
 112: 
 113:     private String label(SignalDecision decision) {
 114:         return decision == null ? "MISSING" : decision.name();
 115:     }
 116: 
 117:     public record Evaluation(boolean exit, String code, String explanation) {
 118:         public static Evaluation exit(String code, String explanation) {
 119:             return new Evaluation(true, code, explanation);
 120:         }
 121: 
 122:         public static Evaluation hold(String explanation) {
 123:             return new Evaluation(false, "HOLD", explanation);
 124:         }
 125: 
 126:         public static Evaluation hold(String code, String explanation) {
 127:             return new Evaluation(false, code, explanation);
 128:         }
 129:     }
 130: }
===== src/main/java/com/crypto/position/service/PositionContinuationPolicy.java =====
   1: package com.crypto.position.service;
   2: 
   3: import com.crypto.domain.SignalDecision;
   4: import com.crypto.domain.TradeSignal;
   5: import lombok.RequiredArgsConstructor;
   6: import org.springframework.stereotype.Component;
   7: 
   8: /**
   9:  * Shared production/replay policy for deciding whether a reached profit target should be extended.
  10:  *
  11:  * IMPORTANT: this class manages an ALREADY-OPEN winner only. It does not loosen BUY generation,
  12:  * entry validation, stop loss, or bearish timeframe vetoes.
  13:  */
  14: @Component
  15: @RequiredArgsConstructor
  16: public class PositionContinuationPolicy {
  17:     /** Minor/moderate immutable-thesis deterioration is tolerated at a TP checkpoint. */
  18:     static final int MAX_CONTINUATION_TREND_PRESSURE = 2;
  19:     /** Momentum must remain essentially intact for the thesis-preservation continuation path. */
  20:     static final int MAX_CONTINUATION_MOMENTUM_PRESSURE = 1;
  21: 
  22:     private final PositionThesisPressurePolicy thesisPressurePolicy;
  23: 
  24:     /**
  25:      * Backward-compatible evaluator used by older unit fixtures. Production/replay should pass
  26:      * the full immutable entry thesis through the overload below.
  27:      */
  28:     public Evaluation evaluate(TradeSignal current, TradeSignal fiveMinute, TradeSignal oneHour,
  29:                                Integer entryTrend, Integer entryMomentum, Integer entryVolume) {
  30:         return evaluate(current, fiveMinute, oneHour,
  31:                 entryTrend, null, entryMomentum, entryVolume, null, null);
  32:     }
  33: 
  34:     public Evaluation evaluate(TradeSignal current, TradeSignal fiveMinute, TradeSignal oneHour,
  35:                                Integer entryTrend, Integer entryStructure,
  36:                                Integer entryMomentum, Integer entryVolume,
  37:                                Integer entryConfidence, Integer entryTotal) {
  38:         if (current == null) return new Evaluation(false, "Continuation FAIL Aú no fresh signal context.");
  39:         if (bearish(current.getDecision()) || (fiveMinute != null && bearish(fiveMinute.getDecision()))
  40:                 || (oneHour != null && bearish(oneHour.getDecision()))) {
  41:             return new Evaluation(false, "Continuation FAIL Aú bearish timeframe detected; normal exit remains active.");
  42:         }
  43: 
  44:         boolean currentSupport = supportive(current.getDecision());
  45:         boolean fiveSupport = fiveMinute != null && supportive(fiveMinute.getDecision());
  46:         boolean fiveNonBearish = fiveMinute != null && !bearish(fiveMinute.getDecision());
  47:         boolean oneSafe = oneHour == null || !bearish(oneHour.getDecision());
  48:         boolean oneBullish = oneHour != null && bullish(oneHour.getDecision());
  49: 
  50:         int trendFloor = Math.max(12, nvl(entryTrend) - 3);
  51:         int momentumFloor = Math.max(7, nvl(entryMomentum) - 4);
  52:         int volumeSoftFloor = Math.max(2, nvl(entryVolume) - 8);
  53: 
  54:         boolean trendHealthy = current.getTrendScore() >= trendFloor;
  55:         boolean momentumHealthy = current.getMomentumScore() >= momentumFloor;
  56:         boolean volumeSupportive = current.getVolumeScore() >= volumeSoftFloor;
  57: 
  58:         // Standard continuation: structure is still supportive. Volume is deliberately a soft
  59:         // confirmation because breakout volume commonly cools while a healthy trend continues.
  60:         boolean standard = currentSupport && fiveSupport && oneSafe
  61:                 && trendHealthy && momentumHealthy
  62:                 && (volumeSupportive || oneBullish);
  63: 
  64:         // Protected HTF continuation: a live 1h BUY may carry a winner through a neutral/cooling
  65:         // 5m phase. It never overrides bearish context and still requires healthy 1m trend/momentum.
  66:         boolean htfProtected = currentSupport && oneBullish && fiveNonBearish
  67:                 && trendHealthy && momentumHealthy;
  68: 
  69:         // Healthy consolidation: low volume is deliberately NOT a hard veto for an already
  70:         // profitable position. If 1m is still WATCH/BUY, the higher timeframes are non-bearish,
  71:         // and trend/momentum remain healthy, cooling volume alone must not force a TP exit.
  72:         boolean healthyConsolidation = currentSupport && fiveNonBearish && oneSafe
  73:                 && trendHealthy && momentumHealthy;
  74: 
  75:         // HTF-supported consolidation: a strong 1h BUY may carry a winner through one neutral
  76:         // 1m candle when 5m remains non-bearish. This is position-management-only; supportive()
  77:         // is intentionally unchanged so entry rules are not loosened.
  78:         boolean currentNonBearish = !bearish(current.getDecision());
  79:         boolean htfSupportedConsolidation = currentNonBearish && !currentSupport
  80:                 && oneBullish && fiveNonBearish && trendHealthy && momentumHealthy;
  81: 
  82:         PositionThesisPressurePolicy.ThesisPressure thesis = thesisPressurePolicy.evaluate(
  83:                 entryTrend, entryStructure, entryMomentum, entryVolume,
  84:                 entryConfidence, entryTotal, current);
  85: 
  86:         // FIX-011 / SOLUSDT 2026-08-19:
  87:         // At TP, SOL had 1m WATCH, 5m NEUTRAL, 1h WATCH and momentum improved 13->15.
  88:         // The old binary trend floor rejected continuation solely because trend fell 21->16
  89:         // (16 < 18), even though PositionManagementService scored the SAME immutable thesis as
  90:         // trend pressure 2/8, momentum pressure 0/5 and HOLD. This narrow path aligns both
  91:         // components: minor thesis cooling may extend a winner, but any bearish timeframe still
  92:         // vetoes above and genuine trend/momentum breakdown still fails these pressure limits.
  93:         boolean thesisIntactConsolidation = currentSupport && fiveNonBearish && oneSafe
  94:                 && thesis.trendPressure() <= MAX_CONTINUATION_TREND_PRESSURE
  95:                 && thesis.momentumPressure() <= MAX_CONTINUATION_MOMENTUM_PRESSURE;
  96: 
  97:         boolean extend = standard || htfProtected || healthyConsolidation
  98:                 || htfSupportedConsolidation || thesisIntactConsolidation;
  99:         String path = htfProtected ? "HTF_TREND"
 100:                 : (standard ? "STANDARD"
 101:                 : (htfSupportedConsolidation ? "HTF_SUPPORTED_CONSOLIDATION"
 102:                 : (healthyConsolidation ? "HEALTHY_CONSOLIDATION"
 103:                 : (thesisIntactConsolidation ? "THESIS_INTACT_CONSOLIDATION" : "NONE"))));
 104:         String checks = "current=" + decision(current)
 105:                 + ", 5m=" + decision(fiveMinute)
 106:                 + ", 1h=" + decision(oneHour)
 107:                 + ", trend=" + current.getTrendScore() + "/" + trendFloor
 108:                 + ", momentum=" + current.getMomentumScore() + "/" + momentumFloor
 109:                 + ", volume=" + current.getVolumeScore() + "/soft " + volumeSoftFloor
 110:                 + ", thesisPressure=" + thesis.trendPressure() + "/8 trend, "
 111:                 + thesis.momentumPressure() + "/5 momentum";
 112: 
 113:         return new Evaluation(extend, extend
 114:                 ? "Continuation PASS (" + path + "): " + checks
 115:                     + ". Volume is advisory during a healthy winner; cooling volume alone does not end the trend."
 116:                 : "Continuation FAIL: " + checks
 117:                     + ". Trend/momentum or higher-timeframe structure no longer supports extending the target; allow normal protection/exit logic.");
 118:     }
 119: 
 120:     private int nvl(Integer v) { return v == null ? 0 : v; }
 121:     private String decision(TradeSignal s) { return s == null || s.getDecision() == null ? "MISSING" : s.getDecision().name(); }
 122:     private boolean supportive(SignalDecision d) { return d == SignalDecision.BUY || d == SignalDecision.STRONG_BUY || d == SignalDecision.WATCH; }
 123:     private boolean bullish(SignalDecision d) { return d == SignalDecision.BUY || d == SignalDecision.STRONG_BUY; }
 124:     private boolean bearish(SignalDecision d) { return d == SignalDecision.SELL || d == SignalDecision.STRONG_SELL; }
 125:     public record Evaluation(boolean extendTarget, String explanation) {}
 126: }
===== src/main/java/com/crypto/position/service/DynamicProfitLockService.java =====
   1: package com.crypto.position.service;
   2: 
   3: import com.crypto.domain.TradeSignal;
   4: import com.crypto.wallet.domain.WalletManagedPosition;
   5: import com.crypto.wallet.domain.WalletSettings;
   6: import com.crypto.wallet.repository.WalletManagedPositionRepository;
   7: import com.crypto.wallet.repository.WalletSettingsRepository;
   8: import lombok.RequiredArgsConstructor;
   9: import lombok.extern.slf4j.Slf4j;
  10: import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;
  11: import org.springframework.transaction.annotation.Transactional;
  12: 
  13: import java.math.BigDecimal;
  14: import java.math.RoundingMode;
  15: import java.time.Instant;
  16: import java.util.Locale;
  17: 
  18: /**
  19:  * Position-manager profit protection for already-open long positions.
  20:  *
  21:  * The BUY/SELL analysis engine is deliberately not involved here. The lock is
  22:  * derived only from the immutable entry price/take-profit stored on the wallet
  23:  * position and the best price observed after entry.
  24:  *
  25:  * Adaptive policy:
  26:  *  - derive an entry-quality score from the immutable entry signal score and confidence;
  27:  *  - stronger setups receive more room before protection activates;
  28:  *  - weaker/medium setups protect profits earlier;
  29:  *  - the protected price can only move upward and never loosens after activation.
  30:  *
  31:  * Administration values remain the fallback when entry-quality information is unavailable.
  32:  */
  33: @Service
  34: @RequiredArgsConstructor
  35: @Slf4j
  36: public class DynamicProfitLockService {
  37: 
  38:     private static final BigDecimal HUNDRED = BigDecimal.valueOf(100);
  39:     private static final int SCALE = 12;
  40: 
  41:     private final WalletManagedPositionRepository positionRepository;
  42:     private final WalletSettingsRepository settingsRepository;
  43:     private final ProfitLockPolicy profitLockPolicy;
  44: 
  45:     @Transactional
  46:     public Evaluation evaluate(TradeSignal signal) {
  47:         if (signal == null) {
  48:             return Evaluation.inactive("No valid market price is available.");
  49:         }
  50:         return evaluatePrice(signal.getSymbol(), signal.getLatestPrice());
  51:     }
  52: 
  53:     /**
  54:      * Evaluates Dynamic Profit Lock directly from a live market price.
  55:      * This path is used by live position protection between candle-close signals.
  56:      *
  57:      * FIX-118 behavioral phase:
  58:      * TP_EXTENSION_REBASE preserves the historically earned lock in persistence but makes
  59:      * it non-executable until progress against the CURRENT TP reaches the configured
  60:      * activation threshold again. This gives an approved continuation meaningful room
  61:      * without forgetting that profit protection had already been earned.
  62:      */
  63:     @Transactional
  64:     public Evaluation evaluatePrice(String symbolValue, BigDecimal currentPrice) {
  65:         if (symbolValue == null || symbolValue.isBlank() || currentPrice == null
  66:                 || currentPrice.signum() <= 0) {
  67:             return Evaluation.inactive("No valid market price is available.");
  68:         }
  69: 
  70:         String symbol = symbolValue.trim().toUpperCase(Locale.ROOT);
  71:         WalletManagedPosition position = positionRepository
  72:                 .findFirstBySymbolAndStatusOrderByOpenedAtDesc(symbol, "OPEN")
  73:                 .orElse(null);
  74:         if (position == null) {
  75:             return Evaluation.inactive("No open wallet position exists.");
  76:         }
  77: 
  78:         WalletSettings settings = settings();
  79:         if (!settings.isDynamicProfitLockEnabled()) {
  80:             return Evaluation.inactive("Dynamic Profit Lock is disabled in Administration.");
  81:         }
  82: 
  83:         BigDecimal entry = position.getAverageEntryPriceUsdt();
  84:         BigDecimal target = position.getTakeProfitUsdt();
  85:         BigDecimal current = currentPrice;
  86:         if (entry == null || target == null || entry.signum() <= 0 || target.compareTo(entry) <= 0) {
  87:             return Evaluation.inactive("The position does not have a valid long take-profit distance.");
  88:         }
  89: 
  90:         ProfitLockProfile profile = configuredProfile(settings);
  91:         BigDecimal activation = profile.activationPercent();
  92:         BigDecimal initialLock = profile.initialLockPercent();
  93:         BigDecimal trailStep = profile.trailStepPercent();
  94:         BigDecimal previousHighest = position.getHighestPriceUsdt();
  95:         boolean previousPersistedActive = position.isProfitLockActive();
  96:         BigDecimal previousLock = position.getProfitLockPriceUsdt();
  97:         BigDecimal previousProgress = position.getProfitLockProgressPercent();
  98:         ProfitLockState previousState = normalizedState(position);
  99: 
 100:         BigDecimal highest = previousHighest == null ? current : previousHighest.max(current);
 101:         BigDecimal progress = progressPercent(entry, target, highest);
 102:         BigDecimal lockPrice = previousLock;
 103:         ProfitLockState state = previousState;
 104:         boolean executableActive;
 105:         boolean triggered;
 106: 
 107:         if (previousState == ProfitLockState.TP_EXTENSION_REBASE && progress.compareTo(activation) < 0) {
 108:             // Keep the legacy active flag/lock persisted so history is not lost, but do not
 109:             // allow the superseded geometry to trigger an exit during the rebase window.
 110:             executableActive = false;
 111:             triggered = false;
 112:         } else {
 113:             // FIX-118 refinement: when a TP-extension rebase earns activation against the
 114:             // CURRENT target, rebuild the executable lock from the current geometry instead
 115:             // of carrying the historical pre-extension lock through ProfitLockPolicy's
 116:             // monotonic previous-lock rule.
 117:             boolean freshGeometryActivation = previousState == ProfitLockState.TP_EXTENSION_REBASE;
 118:             ProfitLockPolicy.State policyState = profitLockPolicy.evaluate(
 119:                     entry, target, current, highest,
 120:                     freshGeometryActivation ? false : (previousState == ProfitLockState.ACTIVE || previousPersistedActive),
 121:                     freshGeometryActivation ? null : previousLock,
 122:                     true, activation, initialLock, trailStep);
 123:             highest = policyState.highestPrice();
 124:             progress = policyState.progressPercent();
 125:             lockPrice = policyState.lockPrice();
 126:             executableActive = policyState.active();
 127:             triggered = policyState.triggered();
 128:             state = executableActive ? ProfitLockState.ACTIVE : ProfitLockState.INACTIVE;
 129:         }
 130: 
 131:         // During REBASE the legacy flag intentionally remains true: protection was historically
 132:         // earned. The explicit state is now the sole execution authority.
 133:         boolean persistedActive = state == ProfitLockState.TP_EXTENSION_REBASE
 134:                 ? true
 135:                 : executableActive;
 136: 
 137:         Instant activatedAt = position.getProfitLockActivatedAt();
 138:         if (persistedActive && activatedAt == null) activatedAt = Instant.now();
 139:         Instant rebaseStartedAt = state == ProfitLockState.TP_EXTENSION_REBASE
 140:                 ? position.getProfitLockRebaseStartedAt()
 141:                 : null;
 142: 
 143:         boolean changed = position.getHighestPriceUsdt() == null
 144:                 || highest.compareTo(position.getHighestPriceUsdt()) != 0
 145:                 || position.isProfitLockActive() != persistedActive
 146:                 || different(position.getProfitLockPriceUsdt(), lockPrice)
 147:                 || different(position.getProfitLockProgressPercent(), progress)
 148:                 || position.getProfitLockState() != state
 149:                 || !java.util.Objects.equals(position.getProfitLockRebaseStartedAt(), rebaseStartedAt)
 150:                 || (position.getProfitLockActivatedAt() == null && activatedAt != null);
 151: 
 152:         if (changed) {
 153:             position.setHighestPriceUsdt(highest);
 154:             position.setProfitLockActive(persistedActive);
 155:             position.setProfitLockPriceUsdt(lockPrice);
 156:             position.setProfitLockProgressPercent(progress);
 157:             position.setProfitLockActivatedAt(activatedAt);
 158:             position.setProfitLockState(state);
 159:             position.setProfitLockRebaseStartedAt(rebaseStartedAt);
 160:             position.setUpdatedAt(Instant.now());
 161:             positionRepository.save(position);
 162:         }
 163: 
 164:         BigDecimal hardProfitFloor = entry.multiply(BigDecimal.valueOf(1.0005));
 165:         boolean hardFloorTriggered = state == ProfitLockState.TP_EXTENSION_REBASE
 166:                 && current.compareTo(hardProfitFloor) < 0;
 167: 
 168:         if (changed || triggered || hardFloorTriggered || previousState != state) {
 169:             log.info("[FIX-118][PRODUCTION][PROFIT_LOCK_EVAL] positionId={}, symbol={}, current={}, entry={}, target={}, " +
 170:                             "previousHighest={}, highest={}, previousState={}, state={}, previousPersistedActive={}, persistedActive={}, " +
 171:                             "previousLock={}, lock={}, previousProgressPct={}, progressPct={}, activationPct={}, triggered={}, " +
 172:                             "hardFloor={}, hardFloorTriggered={}, persisted={}",
 173:                     position.getId(), symbol, current, entry, target,
 174:                     previousHighest, highest, previousState, state, previousPersistedActive, persistedActive,
 175:                     previousLock, lockPrice, previousProgress, progress, activation, triggered,
 176:                     hardProfitFloor, hardFloorTriggered, changed);
 177:         }
 178: 
 179:         String profileText = " Admin profile=" + profile.name() +
 180:                 " (activation=" + activation.stripTrailingZeros().toPlainString() + "%, initial lock=" +
 181:                 initialLock.stripTrailingZeros().toPlainString() + "%, trail=" +
 182:                 trailStep.stripTrailingZeros().toPlainString() + "%).";
 183:         String explanation;
 184:         if (hardFloorTriggered) {
 185:             explanation = "TP-extension rebase is active and price " + current
 186:                     + " breached the protected-profit safety floor " + hardProfitFloor + "." + profileText;
 187:         } else if (state == ProfitLockState.TP_EXTENSION_REBASE) {
 188:             explanation = "Profit Lock is rebasing to the extended take-profit geometry; the historical lock "
 189:                     + lockPrice + " remains persisted but is non-executable. Current best progress is "
 190:                     + progress.setScale(2, RoundingMode.HALF_UP) + "% and reactivation starts at "
 191:                     + activation.stripTrailingZeros().toPlainString() + "%." + profileText;
 192:         } else if (triggered) {
 193:             explanation = "Price " + current + " reached the protected profit-lock level " + lockPrice
 194:                     + " after the position had reached " + progress.setScale(2, RoundingMode.HALF_UP)
 195:                     + "% of its take-profit distance." + profileText;
 196:         } else if (executableActive) {
 197:             explanation = "Profit Lock active at " + lockPrice + "; best progress is "
 198:                     + progress.setScale(2, RoundingMode.HALF_UP) + "% of take-profit distance." + profileText;
 199:         } else {
 200:             explanation = "Profit Lock not active yet; best progress is "
 201:                     + progress.setScale(2, RoundingMode.HALF_UP) + "% and activation starts at "
 202:                     + activation.stripTrailingZeros().toPlainString() + "%." + profileText;
 203:         }
 204: 
 205:         return new Evaluation(true, executableActive, triggered, position.getId(), current, highest,
 206:                 progress, lockPrice, activation, state, hardProfitFloor, hardFloorTriggered, explanation);
 207:     }
 208: 
 209:     /**
 210:      * FIX-120 correction/refinement to FIX-118. Called in the same transaction that persists the new TP.
 211:      * A TP change invalidates the prior executable lock as a calculation baseline. If current
 212:      * best progress remains at/above activation, stay ACTIVE but derive a fresh lock from the
 213:      * new geometry using raw extensionPrice as current and extensionHighest as the historical
 214:      * best seed. If progress falls below activation, preserve the historical lock for audit but
 215:      * make it non-executable in TP_EXTENSION_REBASE until the current geometry requalifies.
 216:      */
 217:     public ExtensionTransition onTakeProfitExtended(WalletManagedPosition position, BigDecimal newTarget, BigDecimal extensionPrice, Instant changedAt) {
 218:         if (position == null || newTarget == null || position.getAverageEntryPriceUsdt() == null) {
 219:             return ExtensionTransition.none();
 220:         }
 221:         ProfitLockState previousState = normalizedState(position);
 222:         BigDecimal oldTarget = position.getTakeProfitUsdt();
 223:         BigDecimal oldLock = position.getProfitLockPriceUsdt();
 224:         BigDecimal extensionHighest = position.getHighestPriceUsdt() == null
 225:                 ? position.getAverageEntryPriceUsdt()
 226:                 : position.getHighestPriceUsdt();
 227:         if (extensionPrice != null && extensionPrice.compareTo(extensionHighest) > 0) {
 228:             extensionHighest = extensionPrice;
 229:             position.setHighestPriceUsdt(extensionHighest);
 230:         }
 231:         BigDecimal progress = progressPercent(position.getAverageEntryPriceUsdt(), newTarget, extensionHighest);
 232:         BigDecimal activation = configuredProfile(settings()).activationPercent();
 233:         ProfitLockState nextState = previousState;
 234:         // TP changed now, so persist progress against the new geometry immediately even though
 235:         // the extension tick returns before the next normal Profit Lock evaluation.
 236:         position.setProfitLockProgressPercent(progress);
 237: 
 238:         if (previousState == ProfitLockState.ACTIVE) {
 239:             if (progress.compareTo(activation) < 0) {
 240:                 nextState = ProfitLockState.TP_EXTENSION_REBASE;
 241:                 position.setProfitLockState(nextState);
 242:                 position.setProfitLockRebaseStartedAt(changedAt == null ? Instant.now() : changedAt);
 243:                 // Legacy compatibility flag deliberately stays true; ProfitLockState is the sole
 244:                 // authority for whether the persisted historical lock may execute.
 245:                 position.setProfitLockActive(true);
 246:             } else {
 247:                 // FIX-120: the extension still leaves the winner beyond activation, but the old
 248:                 // lock belongs to the superseded TP geometry. Establish a fresh geometry baseline.
 249:                 // Deliberately pass raw extensionPrice as current and extensionHighest as previous
 250:                 // highest: ProfitLockPolicy then sees the real boundary tick plus the authoritative
 251:                 // best price, while previousActive/previousLock are intentionally discarded.
 252:                 ProfitLockProfile profile = configuredProfile(settings());
 253:                 BigDecimal currentForPolicy = extensionPrice == null ? extensionHighest : extensionPrice;
 254:                 ProfitLockPolicy.State rebased = profitLockPolicy.evaluate(
 255:                         position.getAverageEntryPriceUsdt(), newTarget, currentForPolicy, extensionHighest,
 256:                         false, null, true, profile.activationPercent(), profile.initialLockPercent(), profile.trailStepPercent());
 257:                 nextState = rebased.active() ? ProfitLockState.ACTIVE : ProfitLockState.TP_EXTENSION_REBASE;
 258:                 position.setHighestPriceUsdt(rebased.highestPrice());
 259:                 position.setProfitLockProgressPercent(rebased.progressPercent());
 260:                 position.setProfitLockPriceUsdt(rebased.lockPrice());
 261:                 position.setProfitLockState(nextState);
 262:                 position.setProfitLockActive(true);
 263:                 position.setProfitLockRebaseStartedAt(nextState == ProfitLockState.TP_EXTENSION_REBASE
 264:                         ? (changedAt == null ? Instant.now() : changedAt)
 265:                         : null);
 266:                 progress = rebased.progressPercent();
 267:             }
 268:         } else if (previousState == ProfitLockState.TP_EXTENSION_REBASE) {
 269:             // Repeated TP extensions keep the rebase active and refresh progress against the
 270:             // newest geometry without resetting the original rebase start timestamp.
 271:         }
 272: 
 273:         log.info("[FIX-120][PRODUCTION][TP_GEOMETRY_REBASE] positionId={}, symbol={}, previousState={}, newState={}, " +
 274:                         "oldTarget={}, newTarget={}, extensionPrice={}, highest={}, newGeometryProgressPct={}, oldLock={}, freshLock={}, " +
 275:                         "activationPct={}, geometryRebased={}",
 276:                 position.getId(), position.getSymbol(), previousState, nextState, oldTarget, newTarget,
 277:                 extensionPrice, position.getHighestPriceUsdt(), progress,
 278:                 oldLock, position.getProfitLockPriceUsdt(),
 279:                 activation, previousState == ProfitLockState.ACTIVE);
 280: 
 281:         return new ExtensionTransition(previousState, nextState, progress, activation);
 282:     }
 283: 
 284:     private ProfitLockState normalizedState(WalletManagedPosition position) {
 285:         if (position.getProfitLockState() != null) return position.getProfitLockState();
 286:         return position.isProfitLockActive() ? ProfitLockState.ACTIVE : ProfitLockState.INACTIVE;
 287:     }
 288: 
 289:     private BigDecimal progressPercent(BigDecimal entry, BigDecimal target, BigDecimal highest) {
 290:         if (entry == null || target == null || highest == null || target.compareTo(entry) <= 0) return BigDecimal.ZERO;
 291:         BigDecimal favorable = highest.subtract(entry);
 292:         if (favorable.signum() < 0) favorable = BigDecimal.ZERO;
 293:         return favorable.multiply(HUNDRED)
 294:                 .divide(target.subtract(entry), 6, RoundingMode.HALF_UP);
 295:     }
 296: 
 297:     @Transactional(readOnly = true)
 298:     public boolean isActive(String symbol) {
 299:         if (symbol == null || symbol.isBlank()) return false;
 300:         return positionRepository.findTopBySymbolAndStatusOrderByOpenedAtDesc(
 301:                         symbol.trim().toUpperCase(Locale.ROOT), "OPEN")
 302:                 .map(p -> normalizedState(p) == ProfitLockState.ACTIVE)
 303:                 .orElse(false);
 304:     }
 305: 
 306: 
 307:     /**
 308:      * Administration is the single source of truth for Dynamic Profit Lock.
 309:      * Entry quality must never silently replace the percentages configured by the user.
 310:      */
 311:     private ProfitLockProfile configuredProfile(WalletSettings settings) {
 312:         return new ProfitLockProfile(
 313:                 "ADMIN_CONFIG",
 314:                 nvl(settings.getProfitLockActivationPercent(), BigDecimal.valueOf(70)),
 315:                 nvl(settings.getProfitLockInitialPercent(), BigDecimal.valueOf(40)),
 316:                 nvl(settings.getProfitLockTrailStepPercent(), BigDecimal.valueOf(10))
 317:         );
 318:     }
 319: 
 320:     private record ProfitLockProfile(
 321:             String name,
 322:             BigDecimal activationPercent,
 323:             BigDecimal initialLockPercent,
 324:             BigDecimal trailStepPercent
 325:     ) {}
 326: 
 327:     private WalletSettings settings() {
 328:         return settingsRepository.findById(1L).orElseGet(() -> settingsRepository.save(
 329:                 WalletSettings.builder()
 330:                         .id(1L)
 331:                         .baseTradeAmountUsdt(BigDecimal.valueOf(100))
 332:                         .minimumUsdtReserve(BigDecimal.ZERO)
 333:                         .maximumDailyNewPositions(0)
 334:                         .performanceWindowType("LAST_TRADES")
 335:                         .performanceTradeCount(20)
 336:                         .performancePeriodDays(1)
 337:                         .dashboardIntervals("1m,5m,1h,4h,1d")
 338:                         .requireNewBuyTransition(true)
 339:                         .executionProfile("BALANCED")
 340:                         .dynamicProfitLockEnabled(true)
 341:                         .profitLockActivationPercent(BigDecimal.valueOf(70))
 342:                         .profitLockInitialPercent(BigDecimal.valueOf(40))
 343:                         .profitLockTrailStepPercent(BigDecimal.valueOf(10))
 344:                         .updatedAt(Instant.now())
 345:                         .build()));
 346:     }
 347: 
 348:     private BigDecimal nvl(BigDecimal value, BigDecimal fallback) {
 349:         return value == null ? fallback : value;
 350:     }
 351: 
 352:     private boolean different(BigDecimal a, BigDecimal b) {
 353:         if (a == null && b == null) return false;
 354:         if (a == null || b == null) return true;
 355:         return a.compareTo(b) != 0;
 356:     }
 357: 
 358:     public record ExtensionTransition(
 359:             ProfitLockState previousState,
 360:             ProfitLockState state,
 361:             BigDecimal progressPercent,
 362:             BigDecimal activationPercent
 363:     ) {
 364:         static ExtensionTransition none() {
 365:             return new ExtensionTransition(ProfitLockState.INACTIVE, ProfitLockState.INACTIVE,
 366:                     BigDecimal.ZERO, BigDecimal.ZERO);
 367:         }
 368:     }
 369: 
 370:     public record Evaluation(
 371:             boolean available,
 372:             boolean active,
 373:             boolean triggered,
 374:             Long walletPositionId,
 375:             BigDecimal currentPrice,
 376:             BigDecimal highestPrice,
 377:             BigDecimal progressPercent,
 378:             BigDecimal lockPrice,
 379:             BigDecimal activationPercent,
 380:             ProfitLockState state,
 381:             BigDecimal hardProfitFloor,
 382:             boolean hardProfitFloorTriggered,
 383:             String explanation
 384:     ) {
 385:         public boolean rebasing() {
 386:             return state == ProfitLockState.TP_EXTENSION_REBASE;
 387:         }
 388: 
 389:         public static Evaluation inactive(String explanation) {
 390:             return new Evaluation(false, false, false, null, null, null,
 391:                     BigDecimal.ZERO, null, null, ProfitLockState.INACTIVE, null, false, explanation);
 392:         }
 393:     }
 394: }
===== src/main/java/com/crypto/position/service/PositionPriceAuthorityPolicy.java =====
   1: package com.crypto.position.service;
   2: 
   3: import com.crypto.domain.TradeSignal;
   4: import org.springframework.stereotype.Service;
   5: 
   6: import java.time.Duration;
   7: import java.time.Instant;
   8: 
   9: /**
  10:  * Single authority for deciding whether a TradeSignal price is temporally valid for
  11:  * price-based position protection (TP / SL / profit-lock / replay mark-to-market).
  12:  *
  13:  * IMPORTANT INCIDENT GUARD (ALLOUSDT 2026-08-17): a 5m signal generated at 18:23:28
  14:  * carried the close of its 18:15-18:19 candle (0.2806). A position opened at 18:23:26
  15:  * at 0.2822 was incorrectly stopped four seconds later because that historical candle
  16:  * close was treated as a current market price. Signal prices remain valid as ANALYSIS
  17:  * CONTEXT, but a candle whose market observation time predates the position may never
  18:  * drive mechanical protection.
  19:  */
  20: @Service
  21: public class PositionPriceAuthorityPolicy {
  22: 
  23:     public boolean canUseSignalPrice(TradeSignal signal, Instant positionOpenedAt) {
  24:         if (signal == null || positionOpenedAt == null || signal.getLatestPrice() == null
  25:                 || signal.getLatestPrice().signum() <= 0) {
  26:             return false;
  27:         }
  28:         Instant observedAt = marketObservationTime(signal);
  29:         return observedAt != null && !observedAt.isBefore(positionOpenedAt);
  30:     }
  31: 
  32:     /** Candle close/observation time, derived from candle open time + interval. */
  33:     public Instant marketObservationTime(TradeSignal signal) {
  34:         if (signal == null || signal.getCandleOpenTime() == null) return null;
  35:         Duration interval = intervalDuration(signal.getInterval());
  36:         return interval == null ? null : signal.getCandleOpenTime().plus(interval);
  37:     }
  38: 
  39:     private Duration intervalDuration(String interval) {
  40:         if (interval == null) return null;
  41:         return switch (interval.trim().toLowerCase()) {
  42:             case "1m" -> Duration.ofMinutes(1);
  43:             case "5m" -> Duration.ofMinutes(5);
  44:             case "15m" -> Duration.ofMinutes(15);
  45:             case "1h" -> Duration.ofHours(1);
  46:             case "4h" -> Duration.ofHours(4);
  47:             case "1d" -> Duration.ofDays(1);
  48:             default -> null;
  49:         };
  50:     }
  51: }
